Asia EM Equity Strategy | Asia Pacific

# Alpha, Beta, Thematic Biweekly

We launch a biweekly briefing on Asia and EM equities, identifying alpha opportunities at the market, sector, thematic and stock level. In this report we explore Asian divergence, India valuations and geopolitical risks. See inside for market performance, valuations, flows & positioning metrics.

## Key Takeaways

What's New: New era dynamics remain in force, supporting Japan and India leadership of Asia/EM equities

■ Market Views: Japan and India remain top DM and EM picks, alongside OWs on Mexico, Poland, Greece & Singapore

– Sector Views: We still like Semis into an expected 2024 recovery, as well as Financials in India & Indonesia and Industrials in India & Mexico

– Key Themes: We like AI Enablers & Adopters, Japanese Corporate Reform and select China SOE & Korea Value opportunities

■ Flows & Positioning: Flows have picked up to China via passive-vehicles, with active US/EU investors still selling. Japan has seen buying but remains UW

Exhibit 1: Chart of the Fortnight – Distribution of 12mf P/E ratios for MSCI India at end Dec-23 vs. MSCI China at end Jan-21; India has broader base of high valuations, where the China market was polarised between value and high growth
![](images/77ce33136f81e01cb1dfb2fef98797bf4893175228499c4953dff39211cf76e3.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research

Top of the Agenda: Asia and EM equity markets have had a highly divergent start to 2024, consistent with our New Era thesis. This looks for structural outperformance of Japan and India, both driven by nominal GDP and a capex/digitalisation agenda, reinforced by multipolar world de-risking trends. Inside we compare Indian valuations with China's early 2021 peak, while highlighting several macro and geopolitical trends. The next key event for Asia/EM is Indonesia's elections on February 14, which may require a run-off Presidential election on June 26. Indonesia equities have historically fallen in the month after elections.

## Top of the Agenda

## Daniel Blake

## New Era Thesis for Japan and India Market Leadership Intact

Asia and EM equity markets have had a highly divergent start to 2024, consistent with our New Era thesis for the asset class (see 2024 Asia EM Equity Outlook: New era for Asia/EM equities, led by Japan and India, November 12, 2023). In this structural thesis, we see:

1. China's earnings base and valuations impacted by the structural headwinds from the "3D" challenges of debt, demographics and deflation in a backdrop of Multipolar World de-risking.

2. Indian equities (our top pick in EM) in contrast supported by these same factors as nominal GDP growth and earnings break out to the upside, while buoyant domestic capital markets support valuations above their historical average premia to EM.

3. Japanese equities (our top pick in DM) benefiting from what we see as a structural return of nominal GDP growth alongside a broadening corporate reform trajectory and domestic digitalisation, automation & de-risking agenda (defence and supply chain spending).

Amid recent developments, we continue to hold a core expectation for Japan and Indian market outperformance versus China and recently adjusted our targets to reflect the incoming earnings and valuation drivers (see Asia EM Equity Strategy: Target price changes for Japan and China, January 25, 2024). Incoming macro and policy signals are yet to show any significant change in the outlook for each of these three markets, as evident in:

1) The recent fall in Chinese government bond yields - reminiscent of the structural downtrend seen in JGB yields through the 1990's, as shown below. Also see China's 3D Journey: Japan vs. China: What Can Be Learned from Japan's ROE Dynamics Since the 1990s (October 15, 2023).

2) India's reacceleration of high-frequency data and positive fiscal surprise.

3) Japan's strong labour market, with positive indications on spring wage negotiations.

Exhibit 2: Comparison of China and Japan bond and equity markets, with a 25 year phase shift – Key to monitor deflationary macro signals for any sustainable turning point in equities
![](images/08e1d92d2a0644e26a714867c93dec6eb8810bd292b37f779dafe1beda50ff30.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

## Contours of India's Valuation Premium

Our sense is that international investors are watching India's equity bull market closely, but are not actively positioned to benefit from the rally. Evidence for this view is found in the EPFR dataset of active fund positioning, which shows that GEM managers have fallen back to benchmark weight on India, equating to the lowest active position on the market since 2007. In contrast, we are bullish on the fundamental and market outlook, with the second largest active allocation behind Japan (see the India Equity Strategy section below for more detail on positioning).

Exhibit 3: GEM Fund Managers' Market Allocation to India vs. MSCI EM Weights - India dipped to underweight, the first time since 2007, given managers have not yet caught up with the rising index weight
![](images/8955ad01f48d86f0361c06583f7b2bd2ffd11d770f08136abcf2c43690d1dd66.jpg)
Source: EPFR Global, Morgan Stanley Research. Data as of December 2023. Note: Monthly data is available on the 24th of the next month

Exhibit 4: GEM Fund Managers' Market Allocation to China vs. MSCI EM Weights - China is seeing dropping benchmark weight and GEM funds managers' allocation, with 100bp underweight versus benchmark
![](images/d0f0586dd60799f5a3b95f10cdf37439e9ae8a8f149cdeb405f0157b32c94f97.jpg)
Source: EPFR Global, Morgan Stanley Research. Data as of December 2023. Note: Monthly data is available on the 24th of the next month

We encounter two key concerns on adding to India at these performance levels - relative valuations and political uncertainty ahead of the Federal elections in April/May. Our colleagues have addressed the election debate, noting that the market is likely to continue rallying into the event in anticipation of continuity and a majority government (see India Equity Strategy: One Billion Voters: Will They Please the Market? September 3, 2023). They note that the Indian market typically approaches elections with optimism, but this would set the market up for post-election volatility, with the potential to swing between +5% and -40% depending on results released in late May.

Turning to valuations, we look beneath the standard headline metrics to assess the breadth of valuation extremes in India. In particular, we compare the Indian market today with the January-February 2021 peak of the MSCI China market, which had moved up by \~7x P/E points (or >60% valuation re-rating) to a peak of \~17.4x versus its prior 10-year average of 10.8x (it currently stands at 8.4x). In contrast, MSCI India at 21.7x is \~3x P/E points (14%) higher than its 10-year average of 19.0x.

Comparing the 12m forward P/E distribution across China at end-January 2021 with India as of end-December 2023, we find a very different profile. China's equity market was much more polarised, with 31.6% of MSCI China constituents trading >200x P/E (or pre-profit) compared with 13.2% for India as at end December 2023. This left the market particularly sensitive to a change in long-run earnings expectations amid the regulatory reset (for the backdrop, see China Macro & Strategy: China's Regulatory Reset, August 2, 2021).

Exhibit 5: Distribution of 12mf P/E ratios for MSCI India at end-Dec 2023 vs. MSC China at end-Jan 2021; India has broader base of high valuations, where the China market was polarised between value and high growth
![](images/350803f08a9d7a02e6834ebebefc61374b2ad730bdc592938bd566e9075439b3.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research

Instead, India's richer valuations are more broad-based across, and within industries, presenting a different set of challenges for active investors. These relate more to traditional investor expertise of identifying companies operating in strong industry structures that are able to deliver sustainable high growth.

Exhibit 6: 12mf P/EMSCI China January 2021 Median 12mf P/E vs. MSCI India December 2023 by GICS Industry Group
![](images/0662479d9ede10d211c47d5e11de565801e94dff197ebdf1cdaf60e71fb5e122.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research

We see India's shifting economic growth model requiring a higher investment trajectory and unlocking a higher profit share of GDP. Structural reforms to accelerate digitalisation and financial deepening and global supply chain diversification are accelerating these trends. As a result, we are now seeing an accelerating trend in trailing earnings for MSCI India, after a decade-long stagnation. While we remain cognisant of relative valuations, we are instead watching these relative earnings trends even more closely, as we see delivery on high growth expectations as the factor most likely to extend India's outperformance.

Exhibit 7: Trailing (actual) EPS for MSCI India vs. MSCI EM - Structural breakout observed, after decade long stagnation
![](images/fbe3457064c34e57132c684363439c9fb5513ed8dfb07016b851e60b62709907.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research, Data as on February 5, 2024

## Geopolitical Backdrop the Key Risk Amid Global Soft-Landing

Energy prices and trade disruptions are again in focus amid broadening tensions in the Middle East and disruptions to shipping lanes through the Red Sea. At this stage, the impact on container freight movement has been mitigated by a timely increase in vessel supply, although our energy analysts have noted a greater impact on European imports of refined oil products (such as diesel and jet fuel). If the situation escalates in a way that threatens global energy supplies, we highlight the fundamental energy balance position of major Asia/EM markets and their recent correlation with oil prices. In particular, Thailand, Korea, Taiwan and South Africa have higher oil and gas trade deficits and therefore any supply shock in energy prices will likely have a larger negative impact on these markets. Australia, Brazil and Malaysia may be net beneficiaries outside of the Middle East.

Exhibit 8: Energy Balance as of GDP versus Index Betas to Oil Prices - Index betas to crude oil prices are positive for most markets, excluding Taiwan
![](images/f5675a997007db8699903de34fb8ab229bb90293d4512a196f02d8f42704c482.jpg)
Source: CEIC, FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research

More broadly, the geopolitical shifts in the Multipolar World transition are a key source of uncertainty, particularly amid a busy election calendar in 2024 (see Asia/EM Catalyst Calendar ). But regardless of the US election outcomes, our thematic view of hegemonic US-China rivalry continues to manifest through an 'economic security dilemma' dynamic of reinforcing national de-risking of supply chains and adjustment to growth models that in many cases exacerbate trade partners' concerns (see Thematics: Practical Guide to a Multipolar World, 13 Jun. 2023). As an example of this continuing trend, recent statements from US Commerce Secretary Raimondo at the Atlantic Council on January 30, 2024 highlighted market and national security distortions with respect to China's competition in EVs, AI and standard-setting bodies.

The intersection between industry technology advances and rising economic security concerns is increasing investor uncertainty in new economy sectors beyond advanced semiconductors. The pharma sector has been in focus since a White House Review of supply chains back in 2021, but the focus on biotechnology has increased over recent years, with the potential for AI to proliferate risks. Market attention was drawn to the issue following a letter on January 31, from the US National Security Commission on Emerging Biotechnology requesting Congressional action to ban the Federal government from procuring products and services from some Chinese firms. While policy is yet to change in that respect, it is important to keep track of the proposals being organised in and around the bipartisan US House Select Committee on Strategic Competition between the United States and the Chinese Communist Party.

## Indonesia the next major election in the EM landscape

Indonesia will hold its first-round presidential elections on February 14, with a run-off to be held on June 26, if none of the three candidates garner >50% of the vote. We have previously noted that MSCI Indonesia tends to slightly outperform prior to the election dates and sees some consolidation afterwards.

Exhibit 9: MSCI Indonesia – Historical performance and valuations around presidential election events

Source: FactSet, RIMES, Morgan Stanley Research. Note performance is calculated to/from the Friday COB price prior to election date

We do not foresee any major shift in policy stance according to the vision documents submitted by the three candidates, but we share the concern of our economists that elections could become drawn-out and extend the period of policy uncertainty, affecting private capex momentum until a cabinet is formed and settles into office later in 3Q24.

Overall, we see Indonesia as relatively attractive within ASEAN (second-ranked, following Singapore), although the market is EW-rated in our APxJ/EM market allocation framework, where it ranks 6th of 27 markets. MSCI Indonesia shows neutral EM-relative valuations versus its five-year history, but superior dividend yields and net profit margins. See Indonesian Equities: 2024 Outlook: What to Own in Indonesia (7 Jan 2024). We prefer Indonesian Financials for their attractive profitability and secular growth opportunity through financial deepening, with Bank Central Asia included in our APxJ and GEM Focus

Lists.

## Asia EM Flows and Positioning

• Aggregate flows (EPFR, to January 31 , 2024): EM and China saw sizeable inflows, but inflows were mainly in passive index benchmarked products, which we think may represent stabilization support of the market by authorities at least partially. Active long-only US and EU funds were still selling Chinese equities ( Exhibit 41 ).

• Flows by market (EPFR, to January 31, 2024): Relative to AUM, China, India and Colombia recorded the largest inflows within EM, while Peru, Mexico and Taiwan recording the largest outflows. ( Exhibit 40 ).

• Japan flows (JPX, to January 26, 2024): Japan foreign inflows have broadly come back since end of October 2023 (despite the selling in the week ending January 26, 2024), having net bought US\$7.8bn YTD 2024. Retailers have been net sellers YTD in aggregate and net sold US\$6.7bn YTD 2024 ( Exhibit 42 and Exhibit 43 ).

• Asia/EM Positioning (EPFR, to December 2023): India dipped to underweight in GEM funds allocation, the first time since 2007, given managers have not yet caught up with the rising index weight ( Exhibit 3 ). China is seeing dropping benchmark weight and GEM funds managers' allocation, with 100bps underweight versus benchmark ( Exhibit 4 ). Japan underweight in global long-only equity funds narrowed to 0.31% as of end-December, and we continue to expect Japan to come to overweight ( Exhibit 45 ).

## Asia Quant Strategy

## Gilbert Wong, Jason Ng

## Key Views

• We recommend investors to stay neutral between Value vs. Growth and prefer Quality.

• For investors with a Value bias, we suggest they may look to take profits from Value stocks if UST bond yields trend higher tactically in 1Q24.

• For investors with longer investment horizons of six to 12 months, we suggest buying any dip on Quality Growth to position for the early stages of a Fed Pivot.

• History suggests market/factor performance around a Fed Pivot would be highly dependent on a soft vs. hard-landing scenario. Our house view is for a softlanding scenario.

• We share the same concern with our economists on the disconnect between market expectations of 6 rate cuts vs. Fed dot plot guidance of 3 cuts, and expect US dollar and UST yields to rise in the near term.

We summarize the average market/style performance in the eight Fed Pivots since the 1970s and focus on the four soft-landing scenarios given our house view expectation – the 1984 Pivot, 1989 Pivot, 1995 Pivot and 2019 Pivot. In a soft-landing scenario where the Fed Pivot was to support economic growth amid a slowdown with inflation moderation, the market tended to perform well before and after the pivot ( Exhibit 47 ).

Exhibit 46: Hard Landing Scenario - Equity Beta Performance
![](images/62060d6859c4493b1046692d12abcc0dee0de3dd45924d559fb5d798136db650.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 47: Soft Landing Scenario - Equity Beta Performance
![](images/c4f3c70d74c20c300b4cb71df97f55821365e6406ed3ce6787c6a1982b4b740f.jpg)

Divergence between Value and Growth style was marginal in soft-landing scenarios ( Exhibit 49 ). We believe this was because Fed policy rates were still high in absolute terms during the early stage of the pivot (>5% in all scenarios except 2019). Duration effect was not yet meaningful in the early stages of the pivot. We believe we will experience a similar situation in the upcoming Fed Pivot.

Exhibit 48: Hard Landing Scenario - Relative return of Value vs. Growth
![](images/b27b11ee0ede1aa65754412b605f9ca111feb30818aff5dd4265140f1f8870bd.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 49: Soft Landing Scenario - Relative return of Value vs. Growth
![](images/c456a09e71a6f85cb6ac640ac09aa78ab8389f66474b08d56c7df25b40839c30.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

We reiterate that Quality exposure is our strategic preferred style given our base case scenario of a soft landing in 2024. Our study shows that Quality stocks tend to outperform the market before and after Fed pivots ( Exhibit 51 ). Quality outperformed in all of the soft-landing and hard-landing scenarios, except 1974.

Exhibit 50: Hard Landing Scenario - Quality factor performance
![](images/8cd42855d0e545be8a65071f0af6c44cbb2e0acd9021e6ad609925f78e184e81.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 51: Soft Landing Scenario - Quality factor performance
![](images/b524ec019d4d6451f3df049c70e0f56e6ab6c663956dd864c67c9ff8c5c2c118.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

From investors' risk sentiment perspective, global asset owners remain risk-off in Asia/EM equities ( Exhibit 52 ), and the risk-allocation is now standing in overly-bearish territory historically - in terms of YTD performance, EM equities continued to underperform DM equities. Gold is trading near all-time highs, while IG bonds outperformed HY bonds. Yet we think the risk-off sentiment is not extreme enough to trigger a reversal of factor performance. According to history, we need to see the indicator below the 15th percentile for four consecutive weeks to see a meaningful factor reversal. For now, we continue to suggest staying patient and use Quality and Revision factors to achieve outperformance.

Exhibit 52: Morgan Stanley Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator vs. MSCI EM – as of February 2, 2024
![](images/434401ea94fc08c1dc7ce4f4a5b81a4636e9b6f79b14665057704a66d941a6be.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, EPFR, MSCI, Morgan Stanley Research. Note: Buy/Sell signals are generated if average weekly readings of the risk sentiment indicator in the last four weeks were below the 15th percentile or above the 85th percentile. The above red/green lines are tracking the forward 6M returns since the signal appeared. MSARSI methodology and its other details can be referred to in insight #1 in the QuantASIA launch report. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

## Japan Equity Strategy

Daniel Blake, Sho Nakazawa, Makoto Furukawa, Jonathan Garner, Kristal Ji

## Key Views

• We see TOPIX moving further into its secular bull market, with our Dec-24 target at 2,800 (+10% from spot).

• We expect further inflows from both foreign and retail investors, positioning for stronger nominal growth and corporate governance.

• Our forecast for JPY appreciation to USD/JPY ¥140 by end-2024 underpins our recommendation for unhedged Japan equity exposure and preference for domestics over exporters

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Mar 19: BoJ Monetary Policy Meeting – We expect the BoJ to hike rates by 10bp in March, exiting Negative Interest Rate Policy

• Mar/Apr: Spring Wage Negotiations – Our economists forecast 3.7-4.0% headline and 2.1-2.4% base-up wage increases for 2024

• Apr 1: BoJ Tankan Survey (Mar)

## Japan Focus List

Today we add OBIC Business Consultants (4733.T), Tokyo Electron (8035.T), Recruit Holdings (6098.T) and UT Group (2146.T) to our Japan Focus List, replacing Nomura Research Institute (4307.T), Denso (6902.T), Hamamatsu Photonics (6965.T) and Resona Holdings (8308.T).

We add OBIC Business Consultants (4733.T) into our Japan Focus List given the expected sustained growth from Bugyo Cloud and effective surplus cash deployment. Our Japan IT Services Analyst Hideaki Tanaka foresees sustained growth as the Bugyo Cloud series gains traction with the firm's deep customer base. The firm's efforts to effectively deploy surplus cash as a means to improve ROE is another powerful catalyst. The cash surplus stood at north of ¥130bn at end-September, and the team look for the firm to strengthen shareholder returns accordingly, mainly on the dividend front. The team's outlook calls for profit growth, with this growth driven by the top line, namely from Bugyo Cloud. For more details see IT & Software　: Attractive Industry View: IT Investment Demand Surging (10 Jan 2024).

We add Tokyo Electron (8035.T) given a bottoming-out SPE market and our Japan Semiconductor Analyst Tetsuya Wadaki expects share of etchers to expand. With improving factory utilization and sales, and the potential to surpass the previous market peak in the next cycle, the team expect the company to enter a phase of seeking new market highs. As the second-largest global player in etching equipment, which is projected to have the highest growth among SPE, we anticipate further share expansion with the introduction of advanced cryo-etching equipment. With a strong sales force and development capabilities driving market share gains in other equipment, the team believe the company is well-positioned for above-average growth in the medium to long term,

warranting an Overweight rating. See Japan Semiconductors: Attractive Industry View Eyeing Next Growth (16 Jan 2024) for more details.

We add Recruit Holdings (6098.T) given market share and profit margins expansion. It is currently our Japan Internet Analyst Tetsuro Tsusaka's top pick and the company has expanded its market share with results-linked billing that was differentiated from job placements billed according to listing numbers, and now it is further enhancing performance transparency. See Recruit Holdings: Advances in Performance-based Billing: Focus on Global Growth (19 Oct 2023) for more details.

We add UT Group (2146.T) given the semi localization trend and growing investment demand. Our Small/Mip-Cap/Non-Manufacturing Industry Analyst Katsumi Arai sees a good chance to price in V-shaped recovery in manufacturing worker dispatch in F3/25. UT Group's share of domestic manufacturing dispatch is lower than other HR subcategories, so the team expect majors to increase market share. The firm has thus been the most aggressive in expanding its share, and the team expect growth in volume (including M&As). The team also look for a pickup in demand for manufacturing personnel as the Japanese government moves to build domestic semiconductor output in the medium term. Earnings slumped in F3/24 on destocking at semiconductor component clients, but demand has already bottomed (in 2Q) and hiring is picking up. F3/24 has been quite tough on the profit side as hiring costs increase, but the team look for a V-shaped recovery in F3/25. See Mid Small Cap/Non-Manufacturer: Japan Small-Mid Cap Stocks: Initiating on Non-manufacturers (Services/Retail) (22 Jan 2024) for more details.

## China Equity Strategy

Laura Wang, Catherine Chen, Raymond Liu

## Key Views:

• We stay relatively cautious given downward earnings revision pressure as the 4Q23 results season unfolds, as well as limited valuation re-rating upside due to the ongoing debt-deflation debates. Our new price targets published on January 25, 2024 - MSCI China 53, Hang Seng 16,000, and CSI300 3,500 suggest 6%, 3% and 9% upside vs. market closing prices as of February 1, 2024. More details here.

• We maintain our preference for A-shares vs. offshore stocks given A-shares' higher concentration with thematic sectors such as high-end manufacturing, green tech, and IT. A-shares also tend to respond more positively towards government easing measures, which we expect to step up gradually during the course of this year.

• Sector-wise, we continue to prefer select Consumption (with preference for Staples), select Materials and Industrials given earnings certainty). We continue to UW Properties and Banks.

• Recommend Morgan Stanley broad China and A-share Best Business Models, China/HK, A-share Thematic Focus Lists and a quant stock screen of better positioned stocks in a deflationary environment.

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Earnings downward revisions to reaccelerate as 2023 annual results are unveiled in February-April 2024, and could cap potential valuation recovery upside.

• Macro data prints: Housing sales, inflation and PMI could help gauge early signs of a macro trough and deflationary pressure clearing up (NBS macro data release schedule). Chinese New Year holiday consumption data (Mar).

• Policy events: National People's Congress (early Mar), Politburo Meeting (late Apr).

• Liquidity: US 10-year bond yield trend and USDCNY trend. Domestic liquidity could remain relatively tight due to lack of credit/monetary multiplier effect [China Equity Strategy: China Free Liquidity vs. MSCI China YoY Change (17 Jan 2024)].

## Australia Macro+ Strategy

Chris Nicol, Chris Read, Antony Conte, Isabel Black

## Key Views:

• Our end-24 price target for the ASX200 is 7350, some 3% below current market levels. Valuations have risen over recent month and are at the top end of their historical relationship with bond yields, which we think puts the emphasis on earnings to drive market upside. While our indicators suggest a trough in earnings is forming, a sharper rebound is not evident at this stage.

• We expect economic growth will remain patchy, and continue to see downside risks across both construction and retail sectors. We do not expect the RBA to pivot to easing as fast as the market expects, and have them on hold through 2024 given sticky services inflation, less restrictive policy settings, and further fiscal stimulus emerging.

• Our recommended sector positioning remains relatively defensive: overweight energy, healthcare and insurance as well as select quality growth and offshore earners names. UW domestic cycle exposure including banks, consumer and housing-linked.

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• February Reporting Season: Our view remains that there is more stock-specific risk after a rally that has lifted most boats - and so expect increased volatility and reaction during this reporting season.

• RBA March Meeting: RBA evolution remains key to the domestic cycle, given the sensitivity of the economy to changes in rates. We continue to see a cautious pivot from the RBA.

• Government May Budget: Government policy remains very active and we see scope for further policy announcements on both the spending and tax sides into the May Budget.

## India Equity Strategy

Ridham Desai, Sheela Rathi, Nayant Parekh

## Key Views:

• India is our key pick across EM markets: The fundamentals are underscored by strong macro stability as a result of improving terms of trade, flexible inflation targeting and stable non-portfolio foreign flows, earnings growth of about 20% annually over the next three to four years led by an emerging private capex cycle, re-leveraging of corporate balance sheets and unfolding of a structural rise in discretionary consumption and a reliable source of domestic risk capital. These factors have reduced both correlations and volatility of Indian stocks relative to EM.

• Base case outlook is for an improving absolute return and outperformance to EM: We expect equities to rise into the 2024 general elections, as the market is likely to price in continuity and a majority government.

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Politics – actions from the opposition alliance, which create a view that it is becoming a credible contender to the incumbent.

• Cues from US stock and bond markets.

• Oil Prices – while India remains less affected than in the past, a further rise would present headwinds.

• Earnings Strength – our base case is 7% and 10% ahead of consensus on F2025 and F2026, respectively.

• Monetary Policy – shallow rate cuts but Fed moves remain uncertain.

• Bond flows further improve BoP.

• Rise in net issuances that could moderate the continuing strong domestic bid.

## Sector Allocation

Cyclicals > Defensives, Large-caps > SMID. OW Financials, Consumer Discretionary, Industrials & Technology and UW other sectors. While we are in a macro market (in contrast to a stock pickers' market) the macro trade could peak in 2024.

## South Korea Equity Strategy

Joon Seok, Heewon Choi, Michelle Kim

## Key Views:

• Government rekindles the capital markets advancement initiative, which has been gaining traction in the market - Korea Musings: Rekindling of Capital Market Advancement Initiative (24 Jan 2024)

We had thought the capital markets reform agenda was briefly in hibernation rather than dead, but the government has rekindled the fire recently with the objective to expand opportunities for wealth accumulation and boosting net wealth effect rather than socioeconomic reforms.

<sub>°</sub> Strong interest in this theme from investors despite some investors remaining skeptical due to the reform agenda not showing progress in past cases.

Activists have increased their activity in the Korean market with Samsung C&T recently in focus and banks still in the cross-hairs – refer to S. Korea Financials: Korean Banks: Activist Investor Carries Out Round 2 of Shareholder Value Boost Drive (12 Jan 2024).

More detailed plans needed to fully comprehend the strategy with the key being execution. Addressing the low valuation in companies reporting framework and changes to share buyback/treasury shares rules are some near-term announcements we are looking for.

• Mid-cycle is coming: We expect mid-cycle transition to come sometime in 1H24 when we expect the market to be able to see clearer signals of policy rate cuts, clarity on geopolitical issues and macro trends. As to where to be positioned when mid-cycle transition nears, conventional wisdom points to non-semi Tech, autos, consumer discretionary and banks - refer to Korea Musings: Cold Front Needs to Recede (16 Jan 2024).

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Elections – April 10, 2024: Unfavorable domestic policy path for domestic livelihood linked stocks and sub-sectors continue to be a drag.

• WGBI Global Bond Index Inclusion Announcement and MSCI Watchlist announcement: MS Views

• Expected lifting of short-sell ban

## Sector Allocation Adjustments:

• Industrials & Banks: Upgrade to OW

• Communication Services: Downgrade to EW

• Energy and Material: Downgrade to UW

## Taiwan Equity Strategy

## Terence Cheng, Jenny Ting

## Key Views:

• We foresee resilient TAIEX performance in 2024 after the presidential overhang is removed, with our Dec-24 target at 18,500 with a bull base target at 20,000.

• We expect bottom-up earnings revisions to further improve as the market expects double-digit growth in 2024 and visibility becomes clearer with end-market inventory destocking cycles for multiple sectors including tech concluded, which should drive a further re-rating of the TAIEX.

• We believe foreign investors are likely to come back and add positions in Taiwan, providing another driver for the index while we expect active domestic investing activities to continue to support the TAIEX.

• We think a fundamental earnings upturn into 2024 along with a leading cash dividend yield vs. regional peers could offer some downside support to its valuation and even drive further upside to valuation if the market seeks both defensive targets and growth stocks.

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Foreign investors participate more actively or add positions in the stock market

• Fed and CBC monetary policy outlook - MS forecasts 100bp Fed cuts in 2024 starting June, with CBC on hold until early 2025

## Sector Allocation:

• OW: Information Technology, Consumer Discretionary, Industrials

• EW: Financials, Consumer Staples

• UW: Materials and Telecoms

# Singapore and ASEAN Equity Strategy

Daniel Blake, Kristal Ji, Wilson Ng, Aitong Li

## Key Views:

• Singapore: Maintain OW on Singapore given high dividend yields and relatively low risks from global slowdown spillovers, with historical outperformance in EM bear markets. 12mf P/E currently stands at 11.4x, 2.1x turns lower than its five-year average.

• Indonesia: Our Asia economist Derrick Kam remains positive on the region's economic prospects and sees domestic demand alpha, combined macro stability with a prudent fiscal and conventional monetary policy mix. Elections and policy continuation remains the key to watch for the Indonesia equity market in the short term. Valuation stands at 13.7x 12mf P/E – lower than its five-year average (14.7x), although positioning is 0.7% overweight in GEM managers' portfolios, slightly higher than the five-year average of 0.5% overweight.

• Philippines: Macro risks persist with a high current account deficit, while foreign participation remains subdued. We recommend cherry picking and our ASEAN Real Estate Analyst Wilson Ng prefers Philippines developers, especially Ayala Land (see ASEAN Property: 2024 Outlook: Watch for falling rates (10 Jan 2024) and ASEAN Multi-Industry: Philippine Titans: Ayala and SM (20 Aug 2023)).

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Indonesia Election – February 14: First-round Presidential elections will be held on 14 February, with a potential run-off on June 26, if no candidate garners >50% of the vote. The vision documents submitted by the three candidates do not seem to suggest a major shift in policy stance. Indonesia does not have a pre-election spending boost, but there are risks that the elections could become drawn-out and extend the period of policy uncertainty, affecting private capex momentum. MSCI Indonesia has historically outperformed prior to the election date and seen consolidation afterwards [Indonesian Equities: 2024 Outlook: What to Own in Indonesia (7 Jan 2024)].

## Latin America Equity Strategy

Nikolaj Lippmann, Juan Ayala, Julia Nogueira

## Key Views:

• We are lowering beta and duration in Brazil after the recent rally. The Brazilian risk reward looks more balanced, especially for some domestic names. We reduced consumer and financial services stocks as well as utilities in our model portfolios.

• We worry that a potential delay on the start of an easing cycle in the US could be a headwind for the pace of further interest rate cuts in Brazil and across the region. Our US economist Ellen Zentner believes the Fed should deliver its first rate cut in June, while the market currently prices in a higher chance of first cut in May (for more details see FOMC Reaction: March Is Out, B/S Discussions to Begin).

• In Latin America, we lean into four key secular themes that can drive superior EPS growth: 1) digitalization (NU, MELI, GLOB, TOTS); 2) near-shoring (GFNORTEO, VESTA, FIBRAPL); 3) energizing the world (PBR, PRIO, ENELCHILE); and 4) expansion of Brazil's agricultural frontiers (RAIL).

• We are overweight Mexico and Brazil. In Mexico, the near-shoring trend should continue to impact manufacturing growth, creating a demand shock for labor, electricity, and manufacturing facilities that in turn will elevate prices and incentivize new investments. In Brazil, we see a very strong case for a longer-term external transformation via the expansion of both its energy and agricultural frontiers, which could add 22-30% to gross exports by the end of the decade, according to our analysts. However, today these are better micro stock-picking themes than macro ones.

• Finally, we are underweight Chile (current account imbalances and high China exposure), Peru (lingering political uncertainty coupled with China exposure), and Colombia (heterodox policy mix).

## Catalysts & Signposts:

• Brazil revenues and spending revision (March 22, 2024): We believe a potential downward revision of the country's fiscal target from a primary deficit of 0.0% of GDP to -0.5% of GDP would signal to markets a loser fiscal policy from the Executive, which could further delay the progression of the current easing cycle.

• Mexico Presidential elections (June 2, 2024): We believe investors will focus on three key factors: 1) energy policy (electricity sector reform and cleanup, and the future vision and priorities around oil company Pemex); 2) fiscal policy (Mexico has been an emerging market standout in recent years, with some late slippage in 2024); and 3) the institutional framework in Mexico that represents.

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of all ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Global Stock Ratings Distribution

## (as of January 31, 2024)

The Stock Ratings described below apply to Morgan Stanley's Fundamental Equity Research and do not apply to Debt Research produced by the Firm.

For disclosure purposes only (in accordance with FINRA requirements), we include the category headings of Buy, Hold, and Sell alongside our ratings of Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight. Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a buy recommendation; we correspond Equal-weight and Not-Rated to hold and Underweight to sell recommendations, respectively.

Stock Ratings: Overweight (0) Equal-weight (E) Underweight (U) Not-Rated (NR) No Rating Available (NA)

Stock and Industry Ratings (abbreviations below) appear as Stock Rating/Industry View

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
